Carries data-amount=\"{minor units}\" |\n| pay-handle, pay-amount, pay-note | Inputs. pay-amount is a **decimal** string as a person would type it, e.g. 15.00 |\n| pay-visibility | Selects public or private. Option values are those two strings |\n| pay-submit | Button |\n| pay-error | Error message, when the payment is refused — including insufficient funds |\n| request-handle, request-amount, request-note, request-submit | The request form |\n| request-error | Error message, when the request is refused |\n\nKeep the pay form's values after success. Submitting it again without changing a field must not send another payment: wallet-balance falls once, the feed contains one payment and pay-error is absent. Changing a field makes the next submission a new payment request. Retries follow §7.\n\n**Formatted amount.** wallet-balance is the decimal with exactly minor_units decimal places, a single space, then the currency code: 100.00 EUR. For a minor_units of 0 there is no decimal point at all: 1200 JPY. Balances are never negative, so there is no sign.\n\nThe form accepts decimal amounts and submits minor units to the API. With minor_units: 2, 15.00 and 15 both submit 1500; 15.5 submits 1550. Nonnumeric input or more than minor_units decimal places must show the form's error element without sending a request. For example, 15.005 is rejected rather than rounded.\n\n## Activity feed — /\n\n| data-testid | Element |\n|---|---|\n| activity-list | Container. Its children are newest first in the DOM |\n| activity-item-{payment_id} | One per visible payment. Present even when the note is empty |\n| empty-activity | Shown instead of the list when nothing is visible |\n\nTwo payments with equal timestamps may appear in either order.\n\n## Requests — /requests\n\n| data-testid | Element |\n|---|---|\n| incoming-list, outgoing-list | Containers |\n| request-item-{request_id} | One per request. Present only on a pending outgoing request |\n| request-error | Shown when a pay, decline or cancel is refused |\n| empty-requests | Shown when both lists are empty |\n\n## Split — /split\n\n| data-testid | Element |\n|---|---|\n| split-amount | Decimal input, same rule as pay-amount |\n| split-handles | Text input: handles separated by commas, in order |\n| split-note, split-submit | Input and button |\n| split-preview | Shows the computed shares before submitting. The preview and submitted split must have identical shares.\n\nAfter any successful action, the balance, the feed and the request lists on the same page must show the new state without a manual reload.       "content": "@[[7f7cdf86-f325-48de-86dd-fd6f97f06cfc]] Handoff part 4 of 5 (the spec needs five parts after all — part 5 is final).\n\n## Competing clients and uncertain outcomes\n\n- Add wallet-refresh, a button on / that refreshes the balance and feed without clearing the pay form. **Latest refresh wins:** a delayed earlier read must not overwrite a later refresh, including when responses arrive out of order.\n- Another client may spend the balance after this browser reads it. A refused payment shows pay-error, refreshes the balance/feed, and preserves all pay inputs. A request cancelled elsewhere while its pay button is visible must show request-error when payment is refused and refresh the request list so the stale pay button disappears.\n- If a payment response is lost, including after POST /payments commits, show pay-uncertain (nonempty text), not pay-error. Keep the unchanged form retryable with the **same key and body**. Successful retry removes both error/uncertainty elements, refreshes the balance and feed, and moves money exactly once. Unknown outcomes are not confirmed rejections.\n\nNo background polling, live synchronization, or recovery across page reloads is required. The same balance refresh rules apply to the available and held amounts introduced below.\n\n## Existing clients after an upgrade\n\nA stage-2 service must accept an export produced by the same team's stage-1 service. A browser signed in before that export/import upgrade must remain signed in afterwards. Existing pending requests remain payable through the request screen. A payment whose response was lost before export remains retryable after import with the same body and key; the UI must recover the original payment and refresh the imported balance. These requirements apply when import completes between browser requests; migration during an in-flight request is not required. No page reload or new screen is required. The form and pending retry identity must survive the upgrade.\n\n## Authorizations and captures\n\nA payment may be **authorised** now and **captured** later, for the full amount or less. An authorisation places a *hold* on the payer's wallet: it reserves money without moving it. Capturing moves the money; a final capture also releases whatever was not captured. Nonfinal captures keep the remainder held. An open authorisation expires and releases its remainder on its own.\n\n1. The sum of all wallet total values always equals the total seeded by the last reset. A hold moves no money; payments, settlements and captures transfer money between wallets.\n2. available = total − held must never be negative. Held funds cannot fund new payments, authorizations or settlement net debits. Captures may spend the money reserved for them.\n3. Cumulative captures must not exceed the authorized amount. Each idempotent capture moves money once. A closed hold cannot be captured again.\n\nThe existing API changes as follows:\n\n- GET /me keeps balance, and balance **equals** total. available and held are new fields beside it. With no open holds, balance, total and available agree and held is zero, and every earlier behaviour is unchanged.\n- POST /payments remains an immediate transfer. It must not leave an intermediate hold or require a separate capture.\n- Every 409 insufficient_funds in stage 1 — on POST /payments, POST /requests/{id}/pay and settlements — is now evaluated against available. With no open holds, the result is unchanged.\n- Paying a request remains immediate. Authorizing a request is out of scope.\n- POST /splits is unchanged.\n- There are now seven idempotent write paths: stage 1's five, authorizations and captures. The same replay rules apply independently to each.",

It defaults to 600 when omitted. If supplied, it must be a positive integer number of seconds. Seeded authorisations carry their own absolute expires_at instead.\n- A user's seeded balance is still total. **available is derived, never seeded** — the service subtracts the seeded open holds itself.\n- A sum of seeded unexpired open holds larger than that user's balance is a reset error: 422 validation_failed from POST /_test/reset, changing nothing, exactly like a negative seeded balance.\n- Seeded status is open, captured, voided or expired. Only open holds anything.\n- An earlier fixture may omit authorizations altogether; omission means an empty list.\n\nAn authorization whose expires_at is at or before now is expired and holds no funds. Reads and writes must reflect expiry even if no request occurred at the deadline. GET /authorizations must show status: \"expired\", and GET /me must include the released remainder in available. Seeded expiry times are at least an hour from reset time, in the past or future; newly created authorizations may have shorter lifetimes.\n\n## API\n\n### GET /me\n\n{ \"user_id\": \"u_ada\", \"display_name\": \"Ada\", \"handle\": \"ada\",\n\"balance\": 10000, \"total\": 10000, \"available\": 8000, \"held\": 2000,\n\"currency\": \"EUR\", \"minor_units\": 2 }\n\nbalance and total are always equal. held is the sum of open holds, and available is total − held, never negative.\n\n### POST /authorizations\n\nIdempotency-Key is required. The caller is the payer.\n\n{ \"to_handle\": \"bob\", \"amount\": 2000, \"note\": \"deposit\", \"visibility\": \"private\" }\n\nnote and visibility are optional with the same defaults as POST /payments.\n\n201\n{\n\"authorization_id\": \"a_4\",\n\"from_user_id\": \"u_ada\", \"from_handle\": \"ada\",\n\"to_user_id\": \"u_bob\", \"to_handle\": \"bob\",\n\"amount\": 2000,\n\"captured_amount\": 0,\n\"currency\": \"EUR\",\n\"note\": \"deposit\",\n\"visibility\": \"private\",\n\"status\": \"open\",\n\"expires_at\": \"2026-09-24T13:20:00+00:00\",\n\"payment_id\": null,\n\"created_at\": \"2026-09-24T13:10:00+00:00\"\n}\n\nexpires_at is created_at plus authorization_ttl_seconds.\n\n| Case | Response |\n|---|---|\n| The caller's available is below amount | 409 insufficient_funds |\n| amount below 1, above 1000000000, or not an integer | 422 validation_failed |\n| to_handle is the caller's own handle | 422 self_payment |\n| note over 200 characters, or visibility neither public nor private | 422 validation_failed |\n| No user has that handle | 404 not_found |\n\nAn open authorisation is **not** a feed item and never appears in GET /activity.",

Only the receiver (the to party) may capture.\n\n{ \"amount\": 1500 }\n\namount is optional and defaults to the authorisation's remaining amount. As on POST /requests/{id}/pay, **a replay must send the identical body** — {} and {\"amount\": 2000} are different JSON values even when they mean the same capture, so reusing a key across the two is 409 idempotency_key_reuse per stage-1.md §7.\n\nReturns 201 with the created **payment**, in exactly the shape POST /payments returns, with authorization_id set to this authorisation and request_id: null. Payments created without an authorisation carry authorization_id: null; their existing request_id semantics are unchanged.\n\nBy default the authorisation becomes captured, carries captured_amount and payment_id, and **releases the uncaptured remainder immediately**: capturing 1500 of 2000 returns 500 to the payer's available in the same step.\n\n**Default: one final capture per authorisation.** A second capture after a final capture is 409 authorization_not_open.\n\n**Extended capture mode.** To keep the remainder held, send {\"amount\": 700, \"final\": false}. final is boolean, default true, so earlier single-capture requests retain their behavior. With final: false and an uncaptured remainder, status stays open; further captures are allowed up to that remainder. Capturing the entire remainder closes it even with final: false. A final capture closes it and releases any remainder. capture_exceeds_authorization compares with the **remaining** amount; omitted amount defaults to that remainder. captured_amount is cumulative; payment_id is the latest capture; payment_ids lists every capture in order. Every authorization response adds remaining_amount: the amount still held, zero when closed. Void and expiry can close a partially captured authorization, release only the remainder, and preserve all capture records. New fields do not change idempotency body equality.\n\n| Case | Response |\n|---|---|\n| The authorisation is not open | 409 authorization_not_open |\n| expires_at is at or before now | 409 authorization_expired |\n| amount above the authorisation's uncaptured remainder | 422 capture_exceeds_authorization |\n| amount below 1, or not an integer | 422 validation_failed |\n| The caller is not the receiver | 403 forbidden |\n| Unknown authorisation | 404 not_found |\n\n### POST /authorizations/{id}/void\n\n**Only the payer may void** — the from party releasing their own hold. No idempotency key, like decline and cancel.\n\n200 with the authorisation, status: \"voided\", the hold released. Voiding an already-voided authorisation is 200 with the current state. A captured or expired one is 409 authorization_not_open.\n\nFor an existing authorization, capture and void return 403 forbidden when the caller is not the permitted party, including callers who are neither party. GET /authorizations returns only authorizations involving the caller.\n\n### GET /authorizations\n\nGET /authorizations?direction=outgoing&status=open&limit=50&offset=0\n\nAuthorisations where the caller is the payer or the receiver, and no others. Newest first by created_at.\n\n- direction is outgoing (the caller is the payer), incoming (the caller is the receiver), or absent for both.\n- status is one of the four statuses, or absent for all. An authorisation expired by the clock matches expired, never open.\n- limit, offset and has_more behave exactly as on GET /requests.\n\n## UI\n\nA new route /authorizations, and the wallet gains two numbers. The UI and the API share /authorizations: serve HTML for Accept: text/html and JSON otherwise, as for /requests.\n\n| data-testid | Element |\n|---|---|\n| wallet-balance | Formatted total, retaining the existing display and data-amount |\n| wallet-available | Formatted available, with data-amount. **Present this as the headline number** — it is what the user can actually spend |\n| wallet-held | Formatted held, with data-amount. Absent when held is zero |\n| authorize-handle, authorize-amount, authorize-note, authorize-visibility, authorize-submit | The authorise form. Same input rules as the pay form |\n| authorize-error | Shown when the authorisation is refused, including insufficient available funds |\n| authorization-list | Container on /authorizations. Children newest first in the DOM |\n| authorization-item-{authorization_id} | Carries data-status=\"{status}\" |\n| authorization-amount-{id} | Text is exactly the formatted authorised amount |\n| authorization-captured-{id} | Formatted captured amount. Present only when status is captured |\n| authorization-expires-{id} | Text is the RFC 3339 expires_at |\n| authorization-capture-amount-{id} | Decimal input, pre-filled with the remaining amount. Present only on an incoming open authorisation |\n| authorization-capture-{id} | Button. Present only on an incoming open authorisation |\n| authorization-void-{id} | Button. Present only on an outgoing open authorisation |\n| authorization-error | Shown when a capture or a void is refused |\n| empty-authorizations | Shown when the list is empty |\n\nThe UI must reflect seeded and newly created holds. The form and pending retry identity must survive the upgrade.\n\n## Authorizations and captures\n\nA payment may be **authorised** now and **captured** later, for the full amount or less. An authorisation places a *hold* on the payer's wallet: it reserves money without moving it. Capturing moves the money; a final capture also releases whatever was not captured. Nonfinal captures keep the remainder held. An open authorisation expires and releases its remainder on its own.\n\n1. The sum of all wallet total values always equals the total seeded by the last reset. A hold moves no money; payments, settlements and captures transfer money between wallets.\n2. available = total − held must never be negative. Held funds cannot fund new payments, authorizations or settlement net debits. Captures may spend the money reserved for them.\n3. Cumulative captures must not exceed the authorized amount. Each idempotent capture moves money once. A closed hold cannot be captured again.\n\nThe existing API changes as follows:\n\n- GET /me keeps balance, and balance **equals** total. available and held are new fields beside it. With no open holds, balance, total and available agree and held is zero, and every earlier behaviour is unchanged.\n- POST /payments remains an immediate transfer. It must not leave an intermediate hold or require a separate capture.\n- Every 409 insufficient_funds in stage 1 — on POST /payments, POST /requests/{id}/pay and settlements — is now evaluated against available. With no open holds, the result is unchanged.\n- Paying a request remains immediate. Authorizing a request is out of scope.\n- POST /splits is unchanged.\n- There are now seven idempotent write paths: stage 1's five, authorizations and captures. The same replay rules apply independently to each.\"\n\nGood. That's part 4 of 5. Then part 5 FINAL.\n\nLet me send.",
